2 Chronicles 1:1

Solomon son of David grew strong over his kingdom. The Lord his God was with him and made him exceedingly great.

  1. 1 Chronicles 29:23-25 Narrative continuation

    The preceding account places Solomon on David's throne, describes Israel submitting to him, and says the Lord made him exceedingly great. This opening resumes that established kingship and divine exaltation.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 29:23 Solomon sat upon the throne of David his father and was accepted with favor. All Israel obeyed him.

    1 Chronicles 29:24 The rulers, the powerful men, and all the sons of King David his father submitted to him.

    1 Chronicles 29:25 The Lord made Solomon exceedingly great before all Israel and gave him a king’s glory such as had not belonged to any king before him.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:2

Solomon spoke to all Israel: the commanders of thousands and hundreds, the judges, and all the rulers before Israel, the ancestral rulers.

  1. 1 Chronicles 28:1 Royal assembly parallel

    The source lists Solomon's commanders of thousands and hundreds among Israel's leaders. David's earlier assembly includes commanders bearing those same numerical ranks and rulers over Israel; the different kings, occasions, and descriptions of other offices remain distinct.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 28:1 David assembled in Jerusalem all Israel’s rulers: the rulers of the judges, the rulers of the service courses attending the king’s person, the commanders of thousands and hundreds, the treasury keepers, those over his possessions, the powerful men, and the army’s warriors.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:3

Solomon and the whole assembly with him went to the high place in Gabaon, where God’s Tent of Testimony was, which Moses, the Lord’s servant, had made in the wilderness.

  1. 1 Chronicles 21:29 Historical sanctuary setting

    Both identify the Lord's tent made by Moses in the wilderness at Gabaon. The earlier location statement explains the sanctuary to which Solomon and the assembly now go.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 21:29 The Lord’s tent, which Moses had made in the wilderness, and the altar of whole burnt offerings were at that time in Bama at Gabaon.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:4

But David had brought God’s ark up from the city of Kariathiarim, because he had prepared a tent for it in Jerusalem.

  1. 1 Chronicles 16:1 Narrative antecedent

    David's prepared tent for the ark is recalled here. The earlier account narrates bringing the ark into the tent David pitched, supplying the antecedent for its separate location from the wilderness tent.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 16:1 They brought God’s ark and set it firmly in the middle of the tent David had pitched for it; they presented whole burnt offerings and offerings of deliverance before God.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:5

The bronze altar that Beseleel son of Ouriou son of Or had made was there before the Lord’s tent, and Solomon and the assembly sought it out.

  1. 1 Chronicles 21:29 Historical altar setting

    The bronze altar stands before the Lord's tent in Solomon's Gabaon visit. The earlier account explicitly places both the wilderness tent and the whole-burnt-offering altar at Gabaon; this connection does not resolve the builder's differently written names elsewhere.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 21:29 The Lord’s tent, which Moses had made in the wilderness, and the altar of whole burnt offerings were at that time in Bama at Gabaon.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:6

Solomon offered there upon the bronze altar before the Lord, the one in the tent; he offered a thousandfold whole burnt offering upon it.

  1. 1 Kings 3:4 Narrative parallel

    Both describe Solomon offering at the high-place altar with a stated thousand: Chronicles calls it a thousandfold whole burnt offering and Kings a thousand whole burnt offerings. The differing formulations are retained.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:4 He rose and went to Gibeon to sacrifice there, for it was the highest place and a great one. Solomon offered a thousand whole burnt offerings on the altar at Gibeon.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:7

That night God appeared to Solomon and said to him, “Ask what I should give you.”

  1. 1 Kings 3:5 Narrative parallel

    Both place God's appearance to Solomon at night and present the invitation to ask for a gift. Kings explicitly calls its appearance sleep; Chronicles' wording is preserved without adding that detail.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:5 The Lord appeared to Solomon in sleep at night. The Lord said to Solomon, “Ask a request for yourself.”

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:8

Solomon said to God, “Thou didst show great mercy to David my father, and Thou didst make me king in his place.

  1. 1 Kings 3:6-7 Narrative prayer parallel

    Solomon acknowledges God's mercy to David and his own appointment as king in David's place in both prayers. Kings expands the account of David's conduct and the son's lack of experience.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:6 Solomon said, “Thou showedst great mercy to Thy servant David my father, as he walked before Thee in truth, righteousness, and uprightness of heart with Thee. Thou keptest this great mercy for him, placing his son on his throne, as it is this day.

    1 Kings 3:7 Now, Lord my God, Thou hast placed Thy servant in place of my father David. I am a little child and do not know my going out or my coming in.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:9

Now, Lord God, let Thy name be confirmed upon David my father, for Thou hast made me king over a people as numerous as the dust of the earth.

  1. 1 Kings 3:7-8 Narrative prayer parallel

    Both prayers join Solomon's appointment in his father's place to the immense size of God's people. Chronicles uses the dust comparison, while Kings says the people cannot be numbered.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:7 Now, Lord my God, Thou hast placed Thy servant in place of my father David. I am a little child and do not know my going out or my coming in.

    1 Kings 3:8 Thy servant is among Thy people whom Thou didst choose, a numerous people who cannot be numbered.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:10

Now give me wisdom and understanding, and I shall go out before this people and come in; for who can judge this great people of Thine?”

  1. 1 Kings 3:9 Narrative prayer parallel

    Solomon asks for the capacity to judge God's great people in both accounts. Wisdom and understanding here correspond to the target's requested hearing heart and discernment for just judgment.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:9 Give Thy servant a heart to hear and to judge Thy people in righteousness, discerning between good and evil; for who can judge this weighty people of Thine?”

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:11

God said to Solomon, “Because this came into your heart, and you did not ask for wealth of possessions, glory, or the life of opponents, and did not ask for many days, but did ask for wisdom and understanding to judge My people over whom I made you king,

  1. 1 Kings 3:11 Narrative response parallel

    Both divine replies contrast Solomon's request for judicial understanding with the wealth, long life, and opponents' lives he did not request. The stated reason for granting the request is shared.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:11 The Lord said to him, “Because you have asked this of Me and have not asked for many days for yourself, or wealth, or the lives of your enemies, but have asked for understanding to hear judgment,

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:12

I give you wisdom and understanding. I will also give you wealth, possessions, and glory, so that none like you has existed among the kings before you, nor will it be so after you.”

  1. 1 Kings 3:12-13 Narrative promise parallel

    Both replies grant unusual wisdom and also unrequested wealth and glory. Kings states the wisdom and royal-wealth promises in separate sentences; Chronicles combines these gifts in its response.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:12 behold, I have done according to your word. Behold, I have given you a discerning and wise heart, so that no one like you has been before you, and no one like you shall arise after you.

    1 Kings 3:13 I have also given you what you did not ask: both wealth and glory, so that no man like you has been among the kings.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:13

Solomon came from the bama in Gabaon to Jerusalem, from before the Tent of Testimony, and he reigned over Israel.

  1. 1 Kings 3:15 Narrative return parallel

    After the divine encounter Solomon comes to Jerusalem in both accounts. Kings continues with offerings and a feast, while Chronicles closes by stating that he reigned over Israel.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 3:15 Solomon awoke: it was a dream. He rose and came to Jerusalem, and stood before the altar that was before the ark of the Lord’s covenant in Zion. He offered whole burnt offerings and peace offerings, and made a great feast for himself and all his attendants.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:14

Solomon gathered chariots and horsemen. He had a thousand four hundred chariots and twelve thousand horsemen. He left them in the chariot cities, and the people were with the king in Jerusalem.

  1. 1 Kings 10:26 Royal military comparison

    Both records give twelve thousand horsemen and place the royal force in chariot cities and Jerusalem. Chronicles separately counts fourteen hundred chariots; Kings counts four thousand mares, and those different categories and figures are not harmonized.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 10:26 Solomon had four thousand mares for chariots and twelve thousand horsemen. He placed the mares in the chariot cities and with the king in Jerusalem.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:15

The king made gold and silver in Jerusalem like stones, and cedars in Judea as abundant as the sycamores in the plain.

  1. 1 Kings 10:27 Verbal royal-abundance parallel

    Both compare Jerusalem's gold and silver to stones and its plentiful cedars to the sycamores of the plain. The paired material comparisons describe Solomon's abundance in the same terms.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 10:27 The king made gold and silver in Jerusalem as common as stones, and cedars as plentiful as the sycamores in the plain.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:16

Solomon’s horses came out of Egypt, and there was the price of the king’s merchants; they bought them to trade,

  1. 1 Kings 10:28 Royal trade parallel

    Both describe Solomon's horses coming from Egypt through royal merchants. Kings additionally names Thekoue; the comparison does not turn Chronicles' price wording into that place-name.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 10:28 Solomon’s horses came from Egypt and from Thekoue; the king’s merchants obtained them from Thekoue in exchange.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:17

and they went up and exported from Egypt one chariot for six hundred in silver and a horse for a hundred fifty. Likewise they brought them through their hands for all the kings of the Chettites and the kings of Syria.

  1. 1 Kings 10:29 Trade parallel with differing prices

    Both describe Egyptian chariot and horse exports and onward trade for foreign kings, including Syria. Chronicles gives six hundred for a chariot and one hundred fifty for a horse; Kings gives one hundred for a chariot and fifty for a horse. The differing prices remain unreconciled.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 10:29 The export from Egypt came up: a chariot for a hundred pieces of silver, and a horse for fifty pieces of silver. In this way they went out by sea for all the Hittite kings and the kings of Syria.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 1:18

Solomon resolved to build a house for the Lord’s name and a house for his kingdom.

  1. 1 Kings 5:19 Temple-building intention

    Both state Solomon's intention to build a house for the Lord's name. Kings connects that intention to the word given to David; Chronicles also mentions the house for Solomon's kingdom.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 5:19 Behold, I intend to build a house for the name of the Lord my God, as the Lord God told my father David: ‘Your son, whom I shall place on your throne in your place, shall build the house for My name.’

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:1

Solomon gathered seventy thousand burden-bearing men and eighty thousand quarrymen in the mountain, with three thousand six hundred overseers over them.

  1. 1 Kings 5:29-30 Labor-force parallel

    Both accounts number seventy thousand burden bearers, eighty thousand mountain stoneworkers, and three thousand six hundred overseers for Solomon's building work. Each stated group and count has an explicit counterpart.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 5:29 Solomon had seventy thousand burden-bearers and eighty thousand stonecutters in the hill country,

    1 Kings 5:30 besides the chiefs appointed over Solomon’s works, three thousand six hundred overseers who performed the works.

2 Chronicles 2:2

Solomon sent to Chiram king of Tyre, saying, “As you dealt with my father David and sent him cedars to build himself a house to live in,

  1. 1 Chronicles 14:1 Explicit historical recollection

    Solomon recalls Chiram king of Tyre supplying David with cedar for a residence. The target narrates that earlier supply and the builders sent to build David's house.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 14:1 Chiram king of Tyre sent messengers to David, cedar wood, wall builders, and carpenters to build him a house.

2 Chronicles 2:3

behold, I his son am building a house for the name of the Lord my God, to consecrate it to Him, to burn incense before Him, with continual presentation and the continual offering of whole burnt offerings morning and evening, on sabbaths, new moons, and the feasts of the Lord our God. This is an everlasting obligation upon Israel.

  1. Numbers 28:3-11 Prescribed worship and temple service

    Solomon names continual morning and evening burnt offerings, sabbaths, and new moons among the temple's duties. Numbers prescribes those daily, sabbath, and new-moon offerings; this pair does not claim that the target also states every other rite listed here.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 28:3 And you shall say unto them: These are the offerings which you shall bring unto the Lord: two unblemished year-old lambs each day for a continual whole burning;

    Numbers 28:4 you shall offer the one lamb in the morning, and you shall offer the second lamb toward evening.

    Numbers 28:5 And you shall prepare a tenth of an oiphi of fine flour for a sacrifice, mixed with oil, a fourth of a hin.

    Numbers 28:6 It is a continual whole burnt offering, instituted at Mount Sinai as a sweet savour unto the Lord.

    Numbers 28:7 And its drink offering shall be a fourth of a hin for each lamb; in the holy place you shall pour out a drink offering of strong drink unto the Lord.

    Numbers 28:8 And you shall offer the second lamb toward evening; you shall prepare it according to its sacrifice and according to its drink offering for a sweet savour unto the Lord.

    Numbers 28:9 And on the Sabbath day you shall bring two unblemished year-old lambs, and two tenths of fine flour mixed with oil for a sacrifice, and a drink offering,

    Numbers 28:10 a Sabbath whole burnt offering on each Sabbath, besides the continual whole burning and its drink offering.

    Numbers 28:11 And at the new moons you shall bring whole burnt offerings unto the Lord: two calves from the cattle, and one ram, and seven unblemished year-old lambs;

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:4

The house I am building is great, for our God is greater than all the gods.

  1. 1 Chronicles 16:25-26 Explicit divine-greatness theme

    Solomon grounds the great house in God's greatness above all gods. The earlier praise likewise declares the Lord great and above the gods, then distinguishes Him as maker of heaven from the nations' idols.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 16:25 For the Lord is great and greatly to be praised; He is fearsome above all the gods.

    1 Chronicles 16:26 For all the nations’ gods are idols, but our God made heaven.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:5

Who will be able to build Him a house, since heaven and the heaven of heaven cannot bear His glory? Who am I to build Him a house, except to burn incense before Him?

  1. 1 Kings 8:27 Explicit sanctuary limitation

    Both Solomon passages say heaven and the heaven of heaven cannot contain or suffice for God, so a constructed house cannot do so. The earlier request limits the house's purpose to worship rather than containing God's glory.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 8:27 But will God truly dwell with people on earth? If heaven and the heaven of heaven are not sufficient for Thee, how much less this house that I have built for Thy name!

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:6

Now send me a wise and knowledgeable man to work in gold, silver, bronze, and iron, in purple, scarlet, and hyacinth blue, skilled at engraving, with the skilled men with me in Judah and Jerusalem whom David my father prepared.

  1. 1 Chronicles 22:15-16 Prepared workforce antecedent

    Solomon requests an additional skilled worker to join the craftsmen David prepared. David's earlier address identifies the abundant stoneworkers, carpenters, and artisans available to Solomon and names gold, silver, bronze, and iron work.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 22:15 With you are workers in abundance: artisans, builders in stone, carpenters in wood, and everyone skilled in every kind of work.

    1 Chronicles 22:16 In gold, in silver, in bronze, and in iron, there is no counting. Arise and act, and the Lord be with you.”

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:7

Send me cedar, juniper, and pine timbers from Lebanon, for I know your slaves know how to cut timber from Lebanon. Behold, your servants will be with my servants

  1. 1 Kings 5:20 Timber-request parallel

    Both requests seek Lebanon timber, acknowledge the other workforce's timber-cutting skill, and arrange cooperation between the two sets of servants. The distinctive working arrangement connects the passages.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 5:20 Now command that they cut timber from Lebanon for me. Behold, my servants are with your servants, and I will pay you the wages of your service according to whatever you say; for you know that no one among us knows how to cut timber like the Sidonians.”

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:8

and will go to prepare abundant timber for me, for the house I am building is great and glorious.

  1. 1 Kings 5:22-24 Request and timber provision

    Solomon's request continues with abundant timber for the great house. The target gives the answering promise of cedar and pine timber and records its supply, connecting the requested building material with the response.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 5:22 He sent to Solomon, saying, “I have heard about everything you did send to me. I will fulfill all your wishes: cedar and pine timber.

    1 Kings 5:23 My servants will bring them down from Lebanon to the sea. I will make them into rafts as far as the place you send me word about, and shake them apart there; you shall take them away. You shall fulfill my wish by providing loaves for my household.”

    1 Kings 5:24 Hiram supplied Solomon with cedars and all that he desired.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:9

Behold, I give the workers cutting timber grain for food, as gifts to your servants: twenty thousand kors, and twenty thousand kors of barley, twenty thousand measures of wine, and twenty thousand measures of oil.”

  1. 1 Kings 5:25 Building-supply exchange comparison

    Both records join the timber arrangement to Solomon's provision of twenty thousand kors of grain and an oil supply. Chronicles lists food for workers and adds barley and wine; Kings describes Hiram's household and different oil terminology, so the allocations are not treated as identical.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 5:25 Solomon gave Hiram twenty thousand kors of wheat and machir for his household, and twenty thousand beth of beaten oil. Solomon gave this to Hiram each year.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:10

Chiram king of Tyre replied in writing and sent it to Solomon: “Because the Lord loves His people, He has appointed you king over them.”

  1. 1 Kings 10:9 Royal appointment and divine love

    Chiram's letter says the Lord appointed Solomon because He loves His people. The queen of Sheba likewise attributes Solomon's appointment over Israel to the Lord's love; the speakers and occasions remain distinct.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 10:9 Blessed be the Lord your God, who delighted in you, setting you on Israel’s throne! Because the Lord loves Israel, to establish them for ever, He made you king over them to carry out judgment in righteousness and in their judgments.”

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:11

Chiram said, “Blessed is the Lord, the God of Israel, who made heaven and earth, who gave King David a wise son, skilled in understanding and knowledge, who will build a house for the Lord and a house for his kingdom.

  1. 1 Kings 5:21 Royal correspondence parallel

    Both replies bless God for giving David a wise or discerning son. Chronicles expands the blessing with creation language and Solomon's building purpose, while Kings reports Hiram's rejoicing at the message.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 5:21 When Hiram heard Solomon’s words, he rejoiced greatly and said, “Blessed be God this day, who has given David a discerning son over this great people!”

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:12

Now I have sent you a wise man, possessing understanding: Chiram, my father,

  1. 2 Chronicles 2:6 Same-chapter request and response

    Solomon requested a wise and knowledgeable craftsman, and the answering letter now says a wise man with understanding has been sent. This connection follows the explicit request and reply without redefining the phrase my father.

    Read matching passage

    2 Chronicles 2:6 Now send me a wise and knowledgeable man to work in gold, silver, bronze, and iron, in purple, scarlet, and hyacinth blue, skilled at engraving, with the skilled men with me in Judah and Jerusalem whom David my father prepared.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:13

his mother is from Dan’s daughters, and his father is a Tyrian man. He knows how to work in gold, silver, bronze, iron, stone, and timber; to weave in purple, hyacinth blue, fine linen, and scarlet; to engrave engravings and devise every design, whatever you give him, with your skilled men and the skilled men of David my lord, your father.

  1. 2 Chronicles 2:6 Same-chapter craftsmanship specifications

    The supplied craftsman's skills include the gold, silver, bronze, iron, colored fabrics, and engraving named in Solomon's request. The reply answers those particular specifications; it does not resolve his maternal identity against another account.

    Read matching passage

    2 Chronicles 2:6 Now send me a wise and knowledgeable man to work in gold, silver, bronze, and iron, in purple, scarlet, and hyacinth blue, skilled at engraving, with the skilled men with me in Judah and Jerusalem whom David my father prepared.

2 Chronicles 2:14

Now let my lord send his servants the grain, barley, oil, and wine of which he spoke.

    The reply asks for the grain, barley, oil, and wine Solomon had offered to the timber workers. The four named provisions identify the earlier offer being accepted.

2 Chronicles 2:15

We will cut timber from Lebanon according to all your need and bring it in rafts to Joppa’s sea. You shall bring it to Jerusalem.”

  1. 1 Kings 5:23 Timber-transport parallel

    Both replies arrange to bring Lebanon timber to the sea as rafts for Solomon to receive. Chronicles specifically names Joppa and the onward journey to Jerusalem; Kings leaves the delivery place to Solomon's instructions.

2 Chronicles 2:16

Solomon gathered all the resident foreign men in Israel’s land after the numbering by which David his father had numbered them. They were found to be a hundred fifty-three thousand six hundred.

  1. 1 Chronicles 22:2 Resident-foreigner labor antecedent

    The census here explicitly follows David's numbering of resident foreigners. The target records David gathering Israel's resident foreigners and appointing quarrymen for God's house; it supplies the earlier workforce setting without claiming to state the later total.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 22:2 David ordered all the resident foreigners in Israel’s land to be gathered. He appointed quarrymen to cut dressed stones for building a house for God.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 2:17

He assigned seventy thousand of them as burden bearers, eighty thousand as quarrymen, and three thousand six hundred as taskmasters over the people.

  1. 2 Chronicles 2:16 Same-chapter assignment and census

    The assigned burden bearers, quarrymen, and taskmasters are the resident foreigners counted immediately before. Their three stated totals sum to the preceding hundred fifty-three thousand six hundred, clarifying the assignment's scope.

    Read matching passage

    2 Chronicles 2:16 Solomon gathered all the resident foreign men in Israel’s land after the numbering by which David his father had numbered them. They were found to be a hundred fifty-three thousand six hundred.

2 Chronicles 3:1

Solomon began building the Lord’s house in Jerusalem on Mount Amoria, where the Lord appeared to David his father, at the place David had prepared, at the threshing floor of Orna the Jebusite.

  1. 1 Chronicles 21:28 Temple-site antecedent

    The source locates the building at Orna the Jebusite's threshing floor, where the Lord appeared to David. The target names that same floor as the place where the Lord answered David and David sacrificed; the shared site connects the accounts while their different verbs remain intact.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 21:28 At that time, when David saw that the Lord had answered him at the threshing floor of Orna the Jebusite, he sacrificed there.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:2

He began building in the second month, in the fourth year of his reign.

  1. 1 Kings 6:1c Building-date parallel

    Both place the beginning or foundation of the Lord's house in Solomon's fourth year and the second month. Kings adds the month's name; the shared year and month are explicit.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 6:1c In the fourth year he laid the foundation of the Lord’s house in the month of Niso, the second month.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:3

These were the measurements when Solomon began building God’s house: the length in cubits, by the first measurement, was sixty cubits, and the width twenty cubits.

  1. 1 Kings 6:2 Temple-dimension comparison

    Both give the Lord's house a width of twenty cubits. Chronicles gives a length of sixty by the first measurement, while Kings gives forty and adds a height; the figures are compared without supplying an unverified reconciliation.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 6:2 The house that King Solomon built for the Lord was forty cubits long, twenty cubits wide, and twenty-five cubits high.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:4

The ailam before the house extended twenty cubits across the house’s width, and its height was a hundred twenty cubits. He gilded it inside with pure gold.

  1. 1 Kings 6:3 Temple-frontage comparison

    The structure before the house spans twenty cubits across the house's width in both accounts. Chronicles additionally gives the ailam's height and gilding, while Kings gives the porch's depth; those different dimensions are not equated.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 6:3 The porch in front of the temple was twenty cubits long across the house’s width, and ten cubits wide in front of the house. He built the house and completed it.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:5

He paneled the great house with cedar wood, gilded it with pure gold, and carved palm trees and hanging ornaments upon it.

  1. 1 Kings 6:29 Temple-decoration parallel

    Both include palm trees in the house's decoration. Chronicles expressly describes carved palms on the paneled and gilded great house, while Kings lists palms in the inner and outer rooms after describing engraved cherubim.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 6:29 On all the house’s walls around he engraved cherubim with a carving tool; palm trees were in the inner and outer rooms.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:6

He adorned the house with precious stones for glory, and with gold from Pharouaim,

  1. 1 Chronicles 29:2 Temple-material preparation and use

    The temple is adorned here with precious stones and gold. David's earlier preparation for God's house explicitly includes gold and every precious stone; the comparison does not identify the source of Pharouaim gold with an unnamed location in the target.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 29:2 With all my strength I have prepared for my God’s house gold, silver, bronze, iron, timber, soom stones and filling stones, costly and variegated stones, every precious stone, and much Parian marble.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:7

and he gilded the house, the walls, gateways, ceilings, and doorways with gold, and carved cherubim upon the walls.

  1. 1 Kings 6:29 Temple-wall carving parallel

    Both explicitly place carved cherubim on the house's walls. Chronicles also enumerates surfaces gilded with gold, while Kings includes palm-tree decoration in its wall description.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 6:29 On all the house’s walls around he engraved cherubim with a carving tool; palm trees were in the inner and outer rooms.

    Pinned source record

2 Chronicles 3:8

He made the House of the Holy of Holies. Its length across the width was twenty cubits, and its width twenty cubits. He gilded it with pure gold for cherubim, amounting to six hundred talents.

  1. 1 Kings 6:20 Inner-sanctuary dimension parallel

    Both describe temple hymn singers using cymbals, nablai, and kinyrai in family-based service. The earlier text supplies the musical organization, while this source adds linen clothing and one hundred twenty trumpeting priests; differing personal spellings are not independently resolved.

    Read matching passage

there was one voice in trumpeting, singing psalms, and shouting, to give thanks and praise to the Lord. When they raised their voice with trumpets, cymbals, and musical instruments, they said, “Give thanks to the Lord, for it is good, for His mercy endures for ever.” The house was filled with a cloud of the Lord’s glory.

  1. 1 Kings 8:10-11 Glory cloud parallel

    Chronicles places the house-filling cloud after united musical thanksgiving. Kings likewise describes the cloud filling the house and identifies it with the Lord's glory, without supplying the musical scene.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 8:10 When the priests came out of the Holy Place, the cloud filled the house.

  2. Psalms 135:1 Liturgical refrain echo

    Both call for thanksgiving to the Lord and ground it in His enduring mercy. Chronicles says it is good, while the psalm says He is kind; the shared refrain is retained with that wording difference.

    Read matching passage

    Both state that the priests could not stand to minister because the cloud and the Lord's glory filled the house.

    Read matching passage

    Pinned source record

  2. Exodus 40:34-35 Sanctuary glory antecedent

    The house filled with glory prevents priestly ministry, recalling the cloud-filled tent that Moses could not enter. The settings and affected ministers differ, while the manifestation and its immediate effect closely correspond.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 40:34 The cloud covered the Tent of Testimony, and the tent was filled with the glory of the Lord.

    Numbers 28:4 you shall offer the one lamb in the morning, and you shall offer the second lamb toward evening.

    Numbers 28:5 And you shall prepare a tenth of an oiphi of fine flour for a sacrifice, mixed with oil, a fourth of a hin.

    Numbers 28:6 It is a continual whole burnt offering, instituted at Mount Sinai as a sweet savour unto the Lord.

    Numbers 28:7 And its drink offering shall be a fourth of a hin for each lamb; in the holy place you shall pour out a drink offering of strong drink unto the Lord.

    Numbers 28:8 And you shall offer the second lamb toward evening; you shall prepare it according to its sacrifice and according to its drink offering for a sweet savour unto the Lord.

2 Chronicles 15:13

  1. Leviticus 25:39-43 Law and practice

    Leviticus 25:42 For these are My household slaves, whom I brought out of the land of Egypt; one shall not be sold with the sale of a household slave.

  1. Luke 10:30-35 Thematic parallel

    Both scenes connect Jericho with mercy toward stripped or injured people, the use of an animal for transport, and restorative care. The comparison does not identify the events or claim an explicit quotation.

    Read matching passage

    Luke 10:32 Likewise a Levite also came to the place, saw him, and passed by on the opposite side.

    Luke 10:33 But a certain Samaritan, traveling, came upon him. When he saw him, he was moved with compassion.

    Deuteronomy 10:13 to keep the commandments of the Lord your God, and His ordinances which I command you this day, that it may be well with you?

2 Chronicles 30:21

The sons of Israel present in Jerusalem kept the Feast of Unleavened Bread for seven days with great rejoicing, hymning the Lord day after day, with the priests and Levites playing instruments for the Lord.

  1. Deuteronomy 16:3-4 Law and practice

    The seven-day Feast of Unleavened Bread follows the prescribed duration of eating unleavened bread and excluding leaven.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 16:3 You shall eat no leaven with it; you shall eat unleavened bread with it for seven days, the bread of affliction, for you came forth out of Egypt in haste; that you may remember the day of your departure out of the land of Egypt all the days of your life.

    Deuteronomy 16:4 No leaven shall be seen with you in all your borders seven days; neither shall any of the flesh which you sacrifice in the evening of the first day remain overnight until the morning.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 28:16-24 Law and practice

    The sustained seven-day celebration corresponds to the law's feast following Passover with offerings each day; the second-month timing comes from the separate postponement provision.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 28:16 And in the first month, on the fourteenth day of the month, is the Lord's Passover.

    Numbers 28:17 And on the fifteenth day of this month is a festival. You shall eat unleavened bread for seven days.

    Numbers 28:18 And the first day shall be a holy appointed assembly for you. You shall do no laborious work.

    Numbers 28:19 And you shall bring whole burnt offerings, offerings unto the Lord: two calves from the cattle, one ram, seven year-old lambs; they shall be unblemished for you.

    Numbers 28:20 And their sacrifice shall be fine flour mixed with oil: three tenths for each calf and two tenths for each ram.

    Numbers 28:21 You shall prepare a tenth for each lamb, for the seven lambs;

    Numbers 28:22 And offer one male goat for sin, to make atonement for you,

    Numbers 28:23 besides the continual morning whole burning, which is a continual whole burnt offering.

    Numbers 28:24 These you shall prepare in this manner each day for seven days, a gift, an offering of sweet savour unto the Lord; besides the continual whole burnt offering you shall prepare its drink offering.

    Ezra 1:1 In the first year of Cyrus king of the Persians, so that the Lord’s word through Jeremiah’s mouth might be fulfilled, the Lord stirred the spirit of Cyrus king of the Persians. He ordered a proclamation throughout his kingdom, also in writing, saying:

2 Chronicles 36:23

“Thus says Cyrus king of the Persians: The Lord, the God of heaven, has given me all the kingdoms of the earth, and He has commanded me to build Him a house in Jerusalem in Judea. Who among you is of all His people? His God shall be with him, and let him go up.”

  1. Isaiah 44:28 Prophecy and fulfillment connection

    Isaiah names Cyrus in connection with rebuilding Jerusalem and laying the foundation of God's house, matching Cyrus's stated commission here.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 44:28 He tells Cyrus to understand, and says, ‘He will do all My wishes.’ He says to Jerusalem, ‘You will be rebuilt, and I will lay the foundation of My holy house.’”

    Pinned source record

  2. Isaiah 45:1-4 Thematic or institutional connection

    The oracle attributes Cyrus's victories and opened kingdoms to Israel's God for Israel's sake, illuminating Cyrus's acknowledgment of divinely given dominion.

    Read matching passage

    Isaiah 45:1 Thus says the Lord God to My anointed Cyrus, whose right hand I grasped so that nations would heed him: “I will shatter kings’ strength, open doors before him, and cities will not be shut.

    Isaiah 45:2 I will go before you and level mountains. I will shatter bronze doors and break iron bars.

    Isaiah 45:3 I will give you dark treasures and open hidden, unseen stores to you, so that you may know that I am the Lord God who calls your name, Israel’s God.

    Isaiah 45:4 For the sake of My servant Jacob and Israel My chosen one, I will call you by your name and welcome you; yet you did not know Me.

  3. Ezra 1:2-3 Narrative parallel

    Ezra preserves Cyrus's acknowledgment of the God of heaven, commission to build the Jerusalem temple, and invitation for God's people to go up, continuing the proclamation that closes Chronicles.

    Read matching passage

    Ezra 1:2 “Thus says Cyrus king of the Persians: The Lord, God of heaven, has given me all the kingdoms of the earth, and He has charged me to build Him a house in Jerusalem in Judea.

    Ezra 1:3 Who among you belongs to any of His people? His God will be with him, and he will go up to Jerusalem in Judea. Let him build the house of the God of Israel—He is the God in Jerusalem.
